                seen.add(c)
                stack.extend(self.parents[c])
        return seen

    def merge_base(self, a, b):                      # a best common ancestor
        common = self.ancestors(a) & self.ancestors(b)
        shadowed = set()                             # ancestors of other common commits
        for c in common:
            for p in self.parents[c]:
                shadowed |= self.ancestors(p)
        best = sorted(common - shadowed)
        return best[0] if best else None

    def log(self, head):                             # children before parents
        reach = self.ancestors(head)
        kids = {c: 0 for c in reach}
        for c in reach:
            for p in self.parents[c]:
                kids[p] += 1
        ready, out = [head], []
        while ready:                                 # Kahn's topological sort
            c = ready.pop(0); out.append(c)
            for p in self.parents[c]:
                kids[p] -= 1
                if kids[p] == 0:
                    ready.append(p)
        return out
--cpp
typedef unsigned long long u64;
u64 mix(u64 x) { x ^= x >> 30; x *= 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9ULL; x ^= x >> 27; x *= 0x94d049bb133111ebULL; return x ^ (x >> 31); }

struct Repo {
    map<string, vector<string>> parents;
    map<string, string> message;

    string commit(const vector<string>& ps, const string& msg) {         // id = hash of parents and message
        u64 h = 0xcbf29ce484222325ULL;
        for (auto& p : ps) for (unsigned char ch : p) h = mix(h ^ ch);
        for (unsigned char ch : msg) h = mix(h ^ ch);
        stringstream ss; ss << hex << h;
        string id = ss.str();
        parents[id] = ps; message[id] = msg;
        return id;
    }

    set<string> ancestors(const string& id) {                            // every commit reachable via parent links
        set<string> seen; vector<string> stack = {id};
        while (!stack.empty()) {
            string c = stack.back(); stack.pop_back();
            if (seen.insert(c).second) for (auto& p : parents[c]) stack.push_back(p);
        }
        return seen;
    }

    string mergeBase(const string& a, const string& b) {                 // a best common ancestor ("" if none)
        set<string> sa = ancestors(a), sb = ancestors(b), common, shadowed;
        set_intersection(sa.begin(), sa.end(), sb.begin(), sb.end(), inserter(common, common.begin()));
        for (auto& c : common) for (auto& p : parents[c]) for (auto& x : ancestors(p)) shadowed.insert(x);   // ancestors of other common commits
        for (auto& c : common) if (!shadowed.count(c)) return c;
        return "";
    }

    vector<string> log(const string& head) {                             // children before parents
        set<string> reach = ancestors(head);
        map<string, int> kids;
        for (auto& c : reach) kids[c] = 0;
        for (auto& c : reach) for (auto& p : parents[c]) kids[p]++;
        deque<string> ready = {head};
        vector<string> out;
        while (!ready.empty()) {                                         // Kahn's topological sort
            string c = ready.front(); ready.pop_front(); out.push_back(c);
            for (auto& p : parents[c]) if (--kids[p] == 0) ready.push_back(p);
        }
        return out;
    }
};
--go
import (
	"fmt"
	"slices"
)

func mix(x uint64) uint64 {
	x ^= x >> 30
	x *= 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9
	x ^= x >> 27
	x *= 0x94d049bb133111eb
	return x ^ x>>31
}

type Repo struct {
	parents map[string][]string
	message map[string]string
}

func newRepo() *Repo { return &Repo{map[string][]string{}, map[string]string{}} }

func (r *Repo) commit(ps []string, msg string) string { // id = hash of parents and message
	h := uint64(0xcbf29ce484222325)
	for _, p := range ps {
		for i := 0; i < len(p); i++ {
			h = mix(h ^ uint64(p[i]))
		}
	}
	for i := 0; i < len(msg); i++ {
		h = mix(h ^ uint64(msg[i]))
	}
	id := fmt.Sprintf("%x", h)
	r.parents[id], r.message[id] = ps, msg
	return id
}

func (r *Repo) ancestors(id string) map[string]bool { // every commit reachable via parent links
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	stack := []string{id}
	for len(stack) > 0 {
		c := stack[len(stack)-1]
		stack = stack[:len(stack)-1]
		if !seen[c] {
			seen[c] = true
			stack = append(stack, r.parents[c]...)
		}
	}
	return seen
}

func (r *Repo) mergeBase(a, b string) string { // a best common ancestor, "" if none
	ancB := r.ancestors(b)
	common := map[string]bool{}
	for c := range r.ancestors(a) {
		if ancB[c] {
			common[c] = true
		}
	}
	shadowed := map[string]bool{} // ancestors of other common commits
	for c := range common {
		for _, p := range r.parents[c] {
			for x := range r.ancestors(p) {
				shadowed[x] = true
			}
		}
	}
	var best []string
	for c := range common {
		if !shadowed[c] {
			best = append(best, c)
		}
	}
	slices.Sort(best)
	if len(best) == 0 {
		return ""
	}
	return best[0]
}

func (r *Repo) log(head string) []string { // children before parents
	reach := r.ancestors(head)
	kids := map[string]int{}
	for c := range reach {
		for _, p := range r.parents[c] {
			kids[p]++
		}
	}
	ready, out := []string{head}, []string{}
	for len(ready) > 0 { // Kahn's topological sort
		c := ready[0]
		ready = ready[1:]
		out = append(out, c)
		for _, p := range r.parents[c] {
			if kids[p]--; kids[p] == 0 {
				ready = append(ready, p)
			}
		}
	}
	return out
}
### autocomplete
--idea
Autocomplete suggests words as you type, and spell check suggests the word you probably meant. Both need to look through a large dictionary fast. A trie (prefix tree) makes the first easy: each node is a letter, and a path from the root spells a prefix. To find every word starting with `ap`, walk down `a`, then `p`, and everything below that node is a completion. The cost depends on the length of the prefix, not on the size of the dictionary.
For mistyped input, the distance between two words is the edit distance: the fewest insertions, deletions and substitutions to turn one into the other. Computing it against every word is slow, but it can be done on the trie in one pass: going down one letter at a time, keep one row of the edit distance table per node, and stop going deeper when the row can no longer produce a small enough distance. A heap that keeps the best `k` results gives the top suggestions by popularity.
--build
1. Trie: each node has a map `children` from a letter to a node, and `weight` for the word that ends here (0 means no word ends here). `insert(word, weight)` walks and creates nodes (see Tries).
2. Prefix completion: walk down the prefix. If a letter is missing, there are no suggestions. Otherwise do a depth-first search below the node collecting every word with its weight.
3. Keep only the best `k`: maintain a min-heap of at most `k` `(weight, word)` pairs. Push each candidate, and if the heap grows beyond `k`, pop the smallest (see Top-k with a heap). At the end sort the survivors from best to worst.
4. Fuzzy search `fuzzy(word, maxd)`: the first row is `0, 1, ..., len(word)` (the cost to turn a prefix of `word` into the empty string). Going down the trie to a child with letter `c`, build the next row: `row[0] = prev[0] + 1`, and for each `j`, `row[j] = min(prev[j] + 1, row[j-1] + 1, prev[j-1] + (word[j-1] != c))`. This is one row of the Edit distance table.
5. If a node ends a word and `row[len(word)] <= maxd`, report the word with that distance. If `min(row) > maxd`, no word below can come within `maxd`, so skip the whole subtree (pruning).
6. Real systems rank suggestions by popularity and recent use, personalise them, and precompute the top `k` for each trie node so a lookup is just a walk.
--think
- Signal: lookups by prefix, "type-ahead", dictionary search with typos. A hash set cannot do prefixes. Sorting the words and using binary search can find a prefix range, but a trie also shares common starts and supports the row trick for fuzzy matching.
- Trade-off: a trie can use a lot of memory (one node per letter). Compressed forms (radix trees, DAWGs, finite state transducers as in Lucene) share both prefixes and endings.
- Precomputing the top `k` at each node makes completion `O(length of prefix + k)` at the cost of memory and slower updates. Many systems do this offline.
- Fuzzy matching speed comes from pruning. With `maxd = 1 or 2` almost all of the trie is cut off after a few letters. A different approach, used by some spell checkers, generates all words within edit distance 1 of the input and looks each up in a hash set.
- Mistakes: forgetting to mark which nodes end a word (so the prefix `app` looks like a word), pushing unlimited results onto the heap instead of capping it at `k`, and letting a case difference or accent make words look different without normalising text first.
--cost
Insert and prefix walk O(length). Collecting completions costs O(size of the subtree + k log k). Fuzzy search costs O(nodes visited * length of the query), and pruning keeps the visited part small.
--java
static class Trie {
    static class Node { Map<Character, Node> kids = new TreeMap<>(); int weight = 0; }
    final Node root = new Node();

    void insert(String word, int weight) {
        Node n = root;
        for (char c : word.toCharArray()) n = n.kids.computeIfAbsent(c, x -> new Node());
        n.weight = weight;
    }

    List<String> complete(String prefix, int k) {                        // best k words with this prefix, best first
        Node n = root;
        for (char c : prefix.toCharArray()) { n = n.kids.get(c); if (n == null) return new ArrayList<>(); }
        PriorityQueue<Object[]> heap = new PriorityQueue<>((a, b) -> {      // min-heap: the worst candidate on top
            int c = Integer.compare((int) a[0], (int) b[0]);
            return c != 0 ? c : ((String) b[1]).compareTo((String) a[1]);
        });
        collect(n, new StringBuilder(prefix), k, heap);
        List<String> out = new ArrayList<>();
        while (!heap.isEmpty()) out.add(0, (String) heap.poll()[1]);
        return out;
    }

    private void collect(Node n, StringBuilder sb, int k, PriorityQueue<Object[]> heap) {
        if (n.weight > 0) { heap.add(new Object[]{n.weight, sb.toString()}); if (heap.size() > k) heap.poll(); }
        for (Map.Entry<Character, Node> e : n.kids.entrySet()) {
            sb.append(e.getKey()); collect(e.getValue(), sb, k, heap); sb.setLength(sb.length() - 1);
        }
    }

    List<String> fuzzy(String word, int maxd) {                          // words within edit distance maxd
        List<String> out = new ArrayList<>();
        int[] row = new int[word.length() + 1];
        for (int j = 0; j < row.length; j++) row[j] = j;
        for (Map.Entry<Character, Node> e : root.kids.entrySet())
            walk(e.getValue(), e.getKey(), new StringBuilder().append(e.getKey()), row, word, maxd, out);
        Collections.sort(out);
        return out;
    }

    private void walk(Node n, char c, StringBuilder sb, int[] prev, String word, int maxd, List<String> out) {
        int[] row = new int[prev.length];
        row[0] = prev[0] + 1;
        int min = row[0];
        for (int j = 1; j < row.length; j++) {                           // one row of the edit distance table
            row[j] = Math.min(Math.min(prev[j] + 1, row[j - 1] + 1), prev[j - 1] + (word.charAt(j - 1) == c ? 0 : 1));
            min = Math.min(min, row[j]);
        }
        if (n.weight > 0 && row[row.length - 1] <= maxd) out.add(sb.toString());
        if (min > maxd) return;                                          // prune: no word below can be close enough
        for (Map.Entry<Character, Node> e : n.kids.entrySet()) {
            sb.append(e.getKey()); walk(e.getValue(), e.getKey(), sb, row, word, maxd, out); sb.setLength(sb.length() - 1);
        }
    }
}
--py
import heapq

class Trie:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kids, self.weight = {}, 0

    def insert(self, word, weight):
        n = self
        for c in word:
            n = n.kids.setdefault(c, Trie())
        n.weight = weight

    def complete(self, prefix, k):                   # best k words with this prefix, best first
        n = self
        for c in prefix:
            n = n.kids.get(c)
            if n is None:
                return []
        heap = []                                    # min-heap of (weight, reversed order key, word): worst on top

        def collect(node, word):
            if node.weight > 0:
                heapq.heappush(heap, (node.weight, [-ord(ch) for ch in word], word))
                if len(heap) > k:
                    heapq.heappop(heap)              # keep only the best k
            for c, child in node.kids.items():
                collect(child, word + c)

        collect(n, prefix)
        return [w for _, _, w in sorted(heap, key=lambda x: (-x[0], x[2]))]

    def fuzzy(self, word, maxd):                     # words within edit distance maxd
        out = []

        def walk(node, c, prefix, prev):
            row = [prev[0] + 1]                      # one row of the edit distance table
            for j in range(1, len(word) + 1):
                row.append(min(prev[j] + 1, row[j - 1] + 1, prev[j - 1] + (word[j - 1] != c)))
            if node.weight > 0 and row[-1] <= maxd:
                out.append(prefix)
            if min(row) <= maxd:                     # prune: no word below can be close enough
                for ch, child in node.kids.items():
                    walk(child, ch, prefix + ch, row)

        first = list(range(len(word) + 1))
        for ch, child in self.kids.items():
            walk(child, ch, ch, first)
        return sorted(out)
--cpp
struct Trie {
    map<char, unique_ptr<Trie>> kids;
    int weight = 0;

    void insert(const string& word, int w) {
        Trie* n = this;
        for (char c : word) { if (!n->kids[c]) n->kids[c] = make_unique<Trie>(); n = n->kids[c].get(); }
        n->weight = w;
    }

    vector<string> complete(const string& prefix, int k) const {         // best k words with this prefix, best first
        const Trie* n = this;
        for (char c : prefix) { auto it = n->kids.find(c); if (it == n->kids.end()) return {}; n = it->second.get(); }
        typedef pair<int, string> P;                                     // better = higher weight, then smaller word
        auto worse = [](const P& a, const P& b) { return a.first != b.first ? a.first > b.first : a.second < b.second; };
        priority_queue<P, vector<P>, decltype(worse)> heap(worse);       // the worst candidate on top
        function<void(const Trie*, const string&)> collect = [&](const Trie* node, const string& word) {
            if (node->weight > 0) { heap.push({node->weight, word}); if ((int)heap.size() > k) heap.pop(); }
            for (auto& kv : node->kids) collect(kv.second.get(), word + kv.first);
        };
        collect(n, prefix);
        vector<string> out;
        while (!heap.empty()) { out.insert(out.begin(), heap.top().second); heap.pop(); }
        return out;
    }

    vector<string> fuzzy(const string& word, int maxd) const {           // words within edit distance maxd
        vector<string> out;
        function<void(const Trie*, char, const string&, const vector<int>&)> walk =
            [&](const Trie* node, char c, const string& prefix, const vector<int>& prev) {
            vector<int> row = {prev[0] + 1};                             // one row of the edit distance table
            for (size_t j = 1; j <= word.size(); j++)
                row.push_back(min({prev[j] + 1, row[j - 1] + 1, prev[j - 1] + (word[j - 1] != c)}));
            if (node->weight > 0 && row.back() <= maxd) out.push_back(prefix);
            if (*min_element(row.begin(), row.end()) <= maxd)            // prune: no word below can be close enough
                for (auto& kv : node->kids) walk(kv.second.get(), kv.first, prefix + kv.first, row);
        };
        vector<int> first(word.size() + 1);
        iota(first.begin(), first.end(), 0);
        for (auto& kv : kids) walk(kv.second.get(), kv.first, string(1, kv.first), first);
        sort(out.begin(), out.end());
        return out;
    }
};
--go
import "sort"

type node struct {
	kids   map[byte]*node
	weight int // > 0 marks the end of a word
}

type Trie struct{ root *node }

func newTrie() *Trie { return &Trie{&node{kids: map[byte]*node{}}} }

func (t *Trie) insert(word string, weight int) {
	n := t.root
	for i := 0; i < len(word); i++ {
		if n.kids[word[i]] == nil {
			n.kids[word[i]] = &node{kids: map[byte]*node{}}
		}
		n = n.kids[word[i]]
	}
	n.weight = weight
}

type cand struct {
	weight int
	word   string
}

func (t *Trie) complete(prefix string, k int) []string { // best k words with this prefix, best first
	n := t.root
	for i := 0; i < len(prefix) && n != nil; i++ {
		n = n.kids[prefix[i]]
	}
	if n == nil {
		return nil
	}
	var all []cand
	var collect func(n *node, w string)
	collect = func(n *node, w string) {
		if n.weight > 0 {
			all = append(all, cand{n.weight, w})
		}
		for c, kid := range n.kids {
			collect(kid, w+string(c))
		}
	}
	collect(n, prefix)
	sort.Slice(all, func(i, j int) bool { // heavier first, ties alphabetical
		return all[i].weight > all[j].weight || all[i].weight == all[j].weight && all[i].word < all[j].word
	})
	var out []string
	for i := 0; i < len(all) && i < k; i++ {
		out = append(out, all[i].word)
	}
	return out
}

func (t *Trie) fuzzy(word string, maxd int) []string { // words within edit distance maxd
	var out []string
	var walk func(n *node, c byte, w string, prev []int)
	walk = func(n *node, c byte, w string, prev []int) {
		row := make([]int, len(prev))
		row[0] = prev[0] + 1
		lowest := row[0]
		for j := 1; j < len(row); j++ { // one row of the edit distance table
			sub := prev[j-1]
			if word[j-1] != c {
				sub++
			}
			row[j] = min(prev[j]+1, row[j-1]+1, sub)
			lowest = min(lowest, row[j])
		}
		if n.weight > 0 && row[len(row)-1] <= maxd {
			out = append(out, w)
		}
		if lowest > maxd { // prune: no word below can be close enough
			return
		}
		for c2, kid := range n.kids {
			walk(kid, c2, w+string(c2), row)
		}
	}
	row := make([]int, len(word)+1)
	for j := range row {
		row[j] = j
	}
	for c, kid := range t.root.kids {
		walk(kid, c, string(c), row)
	}
	sort.Strings(out)
	return out
}

### undo
--idea
A text editor must insert and delete text in the middle of a large file without being slow, and let you undo and redo without limit. Storing the text as one big string makes every edit copy everything after the cursor. A rope fixes this: it stores the text as a binary tree whose leaves are small strings, and each node remembers how many characters are in its subtree. Splitting the text at a position and joining two ropes need only to rebuild the nodes along one path, not copy the text.
Undo comes almost free if the rope is persistent: an edit builds new nodes for the changed path but reuses all the untouched subtrees, so the old version still exists. Keeping an older version is then just keeping a pointer to its root. Undo history is a stack of old roots, and redo is a second stack. VS Code and many editors use related structures (piece tables, piece trees) for the same reason.
--build
1. Node: a leaf holds a string `s`. An inner node holds `left`, `right` and the total length `len`. An empty rope is "nothing" (null). The text is the in-order concatenation of the leaves (see Rope and Tree traversals).
2. `cat(a, b)`: if one side is empty return the other, otherwise make a new inner node with `a` and `b` as children and `len = a.len + b.len`. No text is copied.
3. `split(node, i)`: return the rope of the first `i` characters and the rope of the rest. For a leaf, cut the string into two leaves. For an inner node, if `i <= left.len` split the left child and join the right part with the right child, else split the right child at `i - left.len` and join the left child with the left part. Only nodes on one root-to-leaf path are rebuilt.
4. `insert(i, text)`: split at `i`, then `cat(cat(left, leaf(text)), right)`. `delete(i, j)`: split at `i`, split the remainder at `j - i` and drop the middle, then join the outer parts.
5. Undo and redo use two Stacks of roots. Before each edit, push the current root on `undoStack` and clear `redoStack`. `undo` pushes the current root onto `redoStack` and pops the previous root from `undoStack`. `redo` does the reverse. Because the old trees are never modified, restoring is just swapping a pointer.
6. If you want branching history ("undo tree"), keep the versions as nodes in a doubly linked list or tree instead of two stacks, so you can go back to a state that a new edit would otherwise discard.
--think
- Signal: edits in the middle of a big sequence, with versions. Text editors, collaborative editors, undo for any document model. Persistent data structures share structure between versions, so memory grows with the size of the change, not the size of the document.
- Without balancing, a rope built by many appends becomes a long chain and operations degrade to linear. Real ropes rebalance (AVL or red-black style) or rebuild when too deep, and keep leaf strings large enough (hundreds of bytes) to avoid tiny nodes.
- Alternatives: a gap buffer (a string with a movable gap at the cursor) is simple and fast for local edits, and a piece table stores the original text plus an append-only buffer, with a list of pieces pointing into them. Both are common. Ropes shine for huge files and multi-cursor edits.
- Undo granularity is a product decision: group typing into words or pauses, so one undo removes a sensible chunk. Collaborative editors need operations that can be transformed against others (operational transform or CRDTs), a different problem.
- Mistakes: forgetting to clear the redo stack on a new edit, splitting with an index counted from the wrong side, and not handling empty leaves (they bloat the tree).
--cost
Insert and delete O(depth of the rope), about O(log n) when balanced. Undo and redo O(1). Extra memory per edit is O(depth).
--java
static class Rope {
    final String s; final Rope left, right; final int len;
    Rope(String s) { this.s = s; left = right = null; len = s.length(); }
    Rope(Rope l, Rope r) { s = null; left = l; right = r; len = l.len + r.len; }

    static Rope cat(Rope a, Rope b) { return a == null ? b : b == null ? a : new Rope(a, b); }
    static Rope leaf(String s) { return s.isEmpty() ? null : new Rope(s); }

    static Rope[] split(Rope n, int i) {                         // {first i characters, the rest}
        if (n == null) return new Rope[]{null, null};
        if (n.s != null) return new Rope[]{leaf(n.s.substring(0, i)), leaf(n.s.substring(i))};
        if (i <= n.left.len) { Rope[] p = split(n.left, i); return new Rope[]{p[0], cat(p[1], n.right)}; }
        Rope[] p = split(n.right, i - n.left.len);
        return new Rope[]{cat(n.left, p[0]), p[1]};
    }

    static void text(Rope n, StringBuilder sb) {                 // in-order walk
        if (n == null) return;
        if (n.s != null) sb.append(n.s); else { text(n.left, sb); text(n.right, sb); }
    }
}

static class Editor {
    Rope root = null;
    final Deque<Rope> undo = new ArrayDeque<>(), redo = new ArrayDeque<>();   // stacks of old roots (null = empty text)
    static final Rope EMPTY = new Rope("");

    private void push(Deque<Rope> st, Rope r) { st.push(r == null ? EMPTY : r); }
    private Rope pop(Deque<Rope> st) { Rope r = st.pop(); return r == EMPTY ? null : r; }

    void insert(int i, String text) {
        push(undo, root); redo.clear();
        Rope[] p = Rope.split(root, i);
        root = Rope.cat(Rope.cat(p[0], Rope.leaf(text)), p[1]);
    }
    void delete(int i, int j) {                                  // remove characters i .. j-1
        push(undo, root); redo.clear();
        Rope[] a = Rope.split(root, i), b = Rope.split(a[1], j - i);
        root = Rope.cat(a[0], b[1]);
    }
    void undo() { if (!undo.isEmpty()) { push(redo, root); root = pop(undo); } }
    void redo() { if (!redo.isEmpty()) { push(undo, root); root = pop(redo); } }
    String text() { StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(); Rope.text(root, sb); return sb.toString(); }
}
--py
class Rope:
    def __init__(self, s=None, left=None, right=None):
        self.s, self.left, self.right = s, left, right
        self.len = len(s) if s is not None else left.len + right.len

def leaf(s):
    return Rope(s) if s else None                    # None is the empty rope

def cat(a, b):                                       # no text is copied
    if a is None: return b
    if b is None: return a
    return Rope(left=a, right=b)

def split(n, i):                                     # (first i characters, the rest)
    if n is None:
        return None, None
    if n.s is not None:
        return leaf(n.s[:i]), leaf(n.s[i:])
    if i <= n.left.len:
        a, b = split(n.left, i)
        return a, cat(b, n.right)
    a, b = split(n.right, i - n.left.len)
    return cat(n.left, a), b

def text(n):                                         # in-order walk
    if n is None: return ""
    return n.s if n.s is not None else text(n.left) + text(n.right)

class Editor:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = None
        self.undo_stack, self.redo_stack = [], []    # stacks of old roots; old versions are never modified

    def insert(self, i, s):
        self.undo_stack.append(self.root); self.redo_stack.clear()
        a, b = split(self.root, i)
        self.root = cat(cat(a, leaf(s)), b)

    def delete(self, i, j):                          # remove characters i .. j-1
        self.undo_stack.append(self.root); self.redo_stack.clear()
        a, rest = split(self.root, i)
        _, b = split(rest, j - i)
        self.root = cat(a, b)

    def undo(self):
        if self.undo_stack:
            self.redo_stack.append(self.root)
            self.root = self.undo_stack.pop()

    def redo(self):
        if self.redo_stack:
            self.undo_stack.append(self.root)
            self.root = self.redo_stack.pop()

    def text(self):
        return text(self.root)
--cpp
struct Rope;
typedef shared_ptr<const Rope> RopePtr;                           // null = the empty rope; nodes are never modified
struct Rope {
    string s; RopePtr left, right; int len;
    explicit Rope(string t) : s(move(t)), len(s.size()) {}
    Rope(RopePtr l, RopePtr r) : left(l), right(r), len(l->len + r->len) {}
    bool isLeaf() const { return !left; }
};

RopePtr leaf(const string& s) { return s.empty() ? nullptr : make_shared<Rope>(s); }
RopePtr cat(RopePtr a, RopePtr b) {                               // no text is copied
    if (!a) return b;
    if (!b) return a;
    return make_shared<Rope>(a, b);
}
pair<RopePtr, RopePtr> split(RopePtr n, int i) {                  // {first i characters, the rest}
    if (!n) return {nullptr, nullptr};
    if (n->isLeaf()) return {leaf(n->s.substr(0, i)), leaf(n->s.substr(i))};
    if (i <= n->left->len) { auto p = split(n->left, i); return {p.first, cat(p.second, n->right)}; }
    auto p = split(n->right, i - n->left->len);
    return {cat(n->left, p.first), p.second};
}
void textOf(RopePtr n, string& out) {                             // in-order walk
    if (!n) return;
    if (n->isLeaf()) out += n->s; else { textOf(n->left, out); textOf(n->right, out); }
}

struct Editor {
    RopePtr root;
    vector<RopePtr> undoStack, redoStack;                         // stacks of old roots
    void insert(int i, const string& s) {
        undoStack.push_back(root); redoStack.clear();
        auto p = split(root, i);
        root = cat(cat(p.first, leaf(s)), p.second);
    }
    void erase(int i, int j) {                                    // remove characters i .. j-1
        undoStack.push_back(root); redoStack.clear();
        auto a = split(root, i); auto b = split(a.second, j - i);
        root = cat(a.first, b.second);
    }
    void undo() { if (!undoStack.empty()) { redoStack.push_back(root); root = undoStack.back(); undoStack.pop_back(); } }
    void redo() { if (!redoStack.empty()) { undoStack.push_back(root); root = redoStack.back(); redoStack.pop_back(); } }
    string text() const { string out; textOf(root, out); return out; }
};
--go
import "strings"

type Rope struct { // immutable: a leaf holds text, an inner node joins two ropes
	s           string
	left, right *Rope
	length      int
}

func leaf(s string) *Rope {
	if s == "" {
		return nil
	}
	return &Rope{s: s, length: len(s)}
}

func cat(a, b *Rope) *Rope {
	if a == nil {
		return b
	}
	if b == nil {
		return a
	}
	return &Rope{left: a, right: b, length: a.length + b.length}
}

func split(n *Rope, i int) (*Rope, *Rope) { // the first i characters, the rest
	if n == nil {
		return nil, nil
	}
	if n.left == nil { // leaf
		return leaf(n.s[:i]), leaf(n.s[i:])
	}
	if i <= n.left.length {
		l, r := split(n.left, i)
		return l, cat(r, n.right)
	}
	l, r := split(n.right, i-n.left.length)
	return cat(n.left, l), r
}

func ropeText(n *Rope, sb *strings.Builder) { // in-order walk
	if n == nil {
		return
	}
	if n.left == nil {
		sb.WriteString(n.s)
	} else {
		ropeText(n.left, sb)
		ropeText(n.right, sb)
	}
}

type Editor struct {
	root      *Rope
	undoStack []*Rope // old roots; every edit shares most of its nodes with the old tree
	redoStack []*Rope
}

func (e *Editor) edit(next *Rope) {
	e.undoStack = append(e.undoStack, e.root)
	e.redoStack = nil
	e.root = next
}

func (e *Editor) insert(i int, text string) {
	l, r := split(e.root, i)
	e.edit(cat(cat(l, leaf(text)), r))
}

func (e *Editor) delete(i, j int) { // remove characters i .. j-1
	a, rest := split(e.root, i)
	_, b := split(rest, j-i)
	e.edit(cat(a, b))
}

func (e *Editor) undo() {
	if n := len(e.undoStack); n > 0 {
		e.redoStack = append(e.redoStack, e.root)
		e.root, e.undoStack = e.undoStack[n-1], e.undoStack[:n-1]
	}
}

func (e *Editor) redo() {
	if n := len(e.redoStack); n > 0 {
		e.undoStack = append(e.undoStack, e.root)
		e.root, e.redoStack = e.redoStack[n-1], e.redoStack[:n-1]
	}
}

func (e *Editor) text() string {
	var sb strings.Builder
	ropeText(e.root, &sb)
	return sb.String()
}

### spreadsheet
--idea
In a spreadsheet, a cell can hold a number or a formula that uses other cells, such as `C1 = A1 + B1`. When you change `A1`, every cell that depends on it, directly or through other cells, must be recomputed, and each only after the cells it reads are up to date. The dependencies form a directed graph, and a valid recalculation order is a topological order of it.
Two problems follow. Which cells need recomputing after one edit? Those reachable from the edited cell along "is used by" edges. In what order? Post-order of a depth-first search, reversed, so that every cell comes after the cells it uses. And if a cell depends on itself through a chain (`A1 = B1 + 1` and `B1 = A1`), there is no valid order: it is a cycle, and the spreadsheet shows an error instead of looping forever.
--build
1. Store `cells[name] = formula`. A formula is a sum of terms joined by `+`, each a number or the name of another cell (for example `A1+B1+10`). Real formulas have more operators, but the dependency graph is built the same way: every cell named in a formula is a dependency.
2. Evaluate a cell with a depth-first search that has three states per cell: unvisited, in progress, done (see DFS). `value(name)` marks the cell in progress, evaluates each term (a number as is, a cell by calling `value` on it), and stores the sum as done. Missing cells count as 0.
3. If `value` reaches a cell that is in progress, there is a cycle: raise an error naming the cell (this is Cycle detection, directed).
4. Memoising each finished value means a cell is computed once even if many cells use it, so the time is linear in the size of the sheet, not exponential in the depth.
5. To recompute only what an edit affects: build the reverse graph `usedBy[x]` (the cells whose formula mentions `x`). Run a DFS from the edited cell along `usedBy` edges, and record cells in post-order. The reverse of that list is a valid recalculation order for the affected cells (a Topological sort).
6. Recompute those cells in that order, reading unchanged cells from their stored values. Real spreadsheets also handle ranges (`SUM(A1:A100)`), volatile cells (like the current time), and dirty flags so that a big sheet recomputes only what changed.
--think
- Signal: values defined in terms of other values, "update what depends on what changed". The same structure drives build systems (see Build systems and dependency order), reactive UI frameworks, and incremental computation.
- Pull versus push: evaluating on demand with memoisation (pull) is simple. Pushing changes along reverse edges in topological order (push) avoids recomputing cells nobody looks at, or recomputing a cell twice (the "glitch" when you update `C` before `B` and `C` depends on both).
- Cycles are legitimate to detect, and some spreadsheets allow them with iteration until the values settle. The default is to report an error.
- Recursion depth: a long chain of 100,000 cells makes recursive DFS overflow the stack. Use an explicit stack for production.
- Mistakes: not memoising (an exponential blowup on a diamond of dependencies), forgetting the edited cell's own recomputation, and marking a cell done before its dependencies are finished (which hides cycles).
--cost
Full evaluation O(cells + references). Finding the affected cells after an edit O(affected cells + their references).
--java
static class Sheet {
    final Map<String, String> cells = new HashMap<>();

    void set(String name, String formula) { cells.put(name, formula); }

    Map<String, Integer> evalAll() {
        Map<String, Integer> val = new HashMap<>();
        Set<String> inProgress = new HashSet<>();
        for (String name : cells.keySet()) value(name, val, inProgress);
        return val;
    }

    private int value(String name, Map<String, Integer> val, Set<String> inProgress) {
        if (val.containsKey(name)) return val.get(name);                    // memoised
        if (!cells.containsKey(name)) return 0;                             // a missing cell counts as 0
        if (!inProgress.add(name)) throw new IllegalStateException("cycle at " + name);
        int sum = 0;
        for (String t : cells.get(name).split("\\+")) {
            t = t.trim();
            sum += Character.isDigit(t.charAt(0)) ? Integer.parseInt(t) : value(t, val, inProgress);
        }
        inProgress.remove(name);
        val.put(name, sum);
        return sum;
    }

    List<String> affected(String changed) {                                  // cells to recompute, dependencies first
        Map<String, List<String>> usedBy = new HashMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<String, String> e : cells.entrySet())
            for (String t : e.getValue().split("\\+")) usedBy.computeIfAbsent(t.trim(), k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(e.getKey());
        List<String> post = new ArrayList<>();
        dfs(changed, usedBy, new HashSet<>(), post);
        Collections.reverse(post);                                           // reverse post-order = topological order
        return post;
    }

    private void dfs(String u, Map<String, List<String>> usedBy, Set<String> seen, List<String> post) {
        if (!seen.add(u)) return;
        for (String v : usedBy.getOrDefault(u, Collections.emptyList())) dfs(v, usedBy, seen, post);
        post.add(u);
    }
}
--py
class Sheet:
    def __init__(self):
        self.cells = {}                              # name -> formula such as "A1+B1+10"

    def set(self, name, formula):
        self.cells[name] = formula

    def eval_all(self):
        val, in_progress = {}, set()

        def value(name):
            if name in val:
                return val[name]                     # memoised
            if name not in self.cells:
                return 0                             # a missing cell counts as 0
            if name in in_progress:
                raise ValueError("cycle at " + name)
            in_progress.add(name)
            total = 0
            for t in self.cells[name].split("+"):
                t = t.strip()
                total += int(t) if t.isdigit() else value(t)
            in_progress.discard(name)
            val[name] = total
            return total

        for name in self.cells:
            value(name)
        return val

    def affected(self, changed):                     # cells to recompute, dependencies first
        used_by = {}
        for name, f in self.cells.items():
            for t in f.split("+"):
                used_by.setdefault(t.strip(), []).append(name)
        seen, post = set(), []

        def dfs(u):
            if u in seen:
                return
            seen.add(u)
            for v in used_by.get(u, []):
                dfs(v)
            post.append(u)

        dfs(changed)
        return post[::-1]                            # reverse post-order = topological order
--cpp
struct Sheet {
    map<string, string> cells;                                           // name -> formula such as "A1+B1+10"
    void setCell(const string& name, const string& formula) { cells[name] = formula; }

    static vector<string> terms(const string& f) {
        vector<string> out; string cur; stringstream ss(f);
        while (getline(ss, cur, '+')) { cur.erase(remove(cur.begin(), cur.end(), ' '), cur.end()); out.push_back(cur); }
        return out;
    }

    map<string, int> evalAll() const {
        map<string, int> val; set<string> inProgress;
        function<int(const string&)> value = [&](const string& name) {
            if (val.count(name)) return val[name];                       // memoised
            auto it = cells.find(name);
            if (it == cells.end()) return 0;                             // a missing cell counts as 0
            if (!inProgress.insert(name).second) throw runtime_error("cycle at " + name);
            int sum = 0;
            for (auto& t : terms(it->second)) sum += isdigit((unsigned char)t[0]) ? stoi(t) : value(t);
            inProgress.erase(name);
            return val[name] = sum;
        };
        for (auto& kv : cells) value(kv.first);
        return val;
    }

    vector<string> affected(const string& changed) const {               // cells to recompute, dependencies first
        map<string, vector<string>> usedBy;
        for (auto& kv : cells) for (auto& t : terms(kv.second)) usedBy[t].push_back(kv.first);
        set<string> seen; vector<string> post;
        function<void(const string&)> dfs = [&](const string& u) {
            if (!seen.insert(u).second) return;
            for (auto& v : usedBy[u]) dfs(v);
            post.push_back(u);
        };
        dfs(changed);
        reverse(post.begin(), post.end());                               // reverse post-order = topological order
        return post;
    }
};
--go
import (
	"fmt"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

type Sheet map[string]string // cell name -> formula such as "A1 + B2 + 3"

func (s Sheet) evalAll() (map[string]int, error) {
	val, inProgress := map[string]int{}, map[string]bool{}
	var value func(name string) (int, error)
	value = func(name string) (int, error) {
		if v, ok := val[name]; ok { // memoised
			return v, nil
		}
		f, ok := s[name]
		if !ok { // a missing cell counts as 0
			return 0, nil
		}
		if inProgress[name] {
			return 0, fmt.Errorf("cycle at %s", name)
		}
		inProgress[name] = true
		sum := 0
		for _, t := range strings.Split(f, "+") {
			t = strings.TrimSpace(t)
			v, err := strconv.Atoi(t)
			if err != nil { // not a number: it names another cell
				if v, err = value(t); err != nil {
					return 0, err
				}
			}
			sum += v
		}
		inProgress[name] = false
		val[name] = sum
		return sum, nil
	}
	for name := range s {
		if _, err := value(name); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
	}
	return val, nil
}

func (s Sheet) affected(changed string) []string { // cells to recompute, dependencies first
	usedBy := map[string][]string{}
	for cell, f := range s {
		for _, t := range strings.Split(f, "+") {
			t = strings.TrimSpace(t)
			usedBy[t] = append(usedBy[t], cell)
		}
	}
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	var post []string
	var dfs func(u string)
	dfs = func(u string) {
		if seen[u] {
			return
		}
		seen[u] = true
		for _, v := range usedBy[u] {
			dfs(v)
		}
		post = append(post, u)
	}
	dfs(changed)
	slices.Reverse(post) // reverse post-order = topological order
	return post
}

### searcheng
--idea
A search engine finds the documents that contain your words, and puts the best ones first, without reading every document for every query. The central structure is the inverted index: for each word (term), a list of the documents that contain it, called its posting list, kept sorted by document number. A query such as `red shoes` fetches the lists for `red` and for `shoes` and finds the documents in both.
Finding documents in both is a merge: walk the two sorted lists with a pointer each, like the merge step of merge sort, advancing the smaller one, and keep the document when the two pointers meet. Then each surviving document gets a score. A common one is TF-IDF: the term frequency (how often the word appears in the document) times the inverse document frequency (rarer words count for more). A heap keeps just the top `k` scores, so there is no need to sort every match.
--build
1. Tokenise each document into lowercase words. Build `index[term] = list of (doc, tf)` where `tf` is how many times the term occurs in the doc. Adding documents in increasing id order keeps every list sorted by doc id.
2. Query: tokenise the query and fetch the list for each term. If a term is missing, an AND query has no results.
3. Score each posting: `score = tf * idf`, with `idf = log(1 + N / df)`, where `N` is the number of documents and `df` is the length of that term's list (how many documents have the term).
4. AND the lists with a merge (see Merge sort for the merge idea). Take the two lists with the pointers `i` and `j`: if the doc ids are equal, output the doc with the sum of the two scores and advance both, else advance the one with the smaller doc id. For more than two terms, merge two at a time, starting with the shortest lists so intermediate results stay small.
5. Keep the best `k` of the merged results with a min-heap of size `k` (Top-k with a heap): push each result and pop the smallest when the heap is larger than `k`. Output from best to worst.
6. A trie or a sorted dictionary maps each query term to its posting list (see Tries). Real engines also compress the lists (store gaps between doc ids), use skip pointers to jump over long runs, support phrases by storing word positions, and add ranking signals beyond TF-IDF such as BM25 and link analysis.
--think
- Signal: "find documents by words", filtering and ranking of text at scale. The inverted index turns a question about documents into a question about words, which is why it is fast: query cost depends on the lengths of the lists involved, not on the whole collection.
- AND merges favour rare words: begin with the shortest list. OR queries merge by taking the union and adding scores. Phrase queries check that positions are consecutive.
- At scale: the index is split by document into shards, each shard returns its own top `k`, and a coordinator merges those lists with another heap. Hot index pieces are kept in memory (see CPU, web and database caches).
- Ranking matters as much as retrieval. TF-IDF is a baseline. BM25 adds length normalisation and saturating term frequency, and modern engines add learned ranking and query understanding.
- Mistakes: unsorted posting lists (the merge silently gives wrong answers), computing `idf` from the filtered results instead of the whole collection, and sorting all matches when only the top few are needed.
--cost
Building the index is O(total words). An AND query costs O(sum of the lengths of the lists merged) and the heap adds O(matches * log k).
--java
static Map<String, List<int[]>> buildIndex(List<String> docs) {            // term -> list of {doc, tf}, sorted by doc
    Map<String, List<int[]>> index = new HashMap<>();
    for (int d = 0; d < docs.size(); d++) {
        Map<String, Integer> tf = new HashMap<>();
        for (String w : docs.get(d).toLowerCase().split("\\s+")) tf.merge(w, 1, Integer::sum);
        for (Map.Entry<String, Integer> e : tf.entrySet())
            index.computeIfAbsent(e.getKey(), k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(new int[]{d, e.getValue()});
    }
    return index;
}

static List<double[]> search(Map<String, List<int[]>> index, int nDocs, String query, int k) {   // returns {doc, score}
    List<List<double[]>> lists = new ArrayList<>();
    for (String t : query.toLowerCase().split("\\s+")) {
        List<int[]> post = index.get(t);
        if (post == null) return new ArrayList<>();                         // AND: a missing term means no results
        double idf = Math.log(1.0 + (double) nDocs / post.size());
        List<double[]> scored = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int[] p : post) scored.add(new double[]{p[0], p[1] * idf});    // tf * idf
        lists.add(scored);
    }
    lists.sort(Comparator.comparingInt(List::size));                        // shortest list first
    List<double[]> cur = lists.get(0);
    for (int x = 1; x < lists.size(); x++) cur = mergeAnd(cur, lists.get(x));
    PriorityQueue<double[]> heap = new PriorityQueue<>((a, b) -> a[1] != b[1] ? Double.compare(a[1], b[1]) : Double.compare(b[0], a[0]));
    for (double[] r : cur) { heap.add(r); if (heap.size() > k) heap.poll(); }   // keep the best k
    List<double[]> out = new ArrayList<>();
    while (!heap.isEmpty()) out.add(0, heap.poll());
    return out;
}

static List<double[]> mergeAnd(List<double[]> a, List<double[]> b) {        // two-pointer merge of sorted lists
    List<double[]> out = new ArrayList<>();
    int i = 0, j = 0;
    while (i < a.size() && j < b.size()) {
        if (a.get(i)[0] == b.get(j)[0]) { out.add(new double[]{a.get(i)[0], a.get(i)[1] + b.get(j)[1]}); i++; j++; }
        else if (a.get(i)[0] < b.get(j)[0]) i++;
        else j++;
    }
    return out;
}
--py
import heapq, math

def build_index(docs):                               # term -> list of (doc, tf), sorted by doc
    index = {}
    for d, text in enumerate(docs):
        tf = {}
        for w in text.lower().split():
            tf[w] = tf.get(w, 0) + 1
        for w, c in tf.items():
            index.setdefault(w, []).append((d, c))
    return index

def merge_and(a, b):                                 # two-pointer merge of lists of (doc, score) sorted by doc
    out, i, j = [], 0, 0
    while i < len(a) and j < len(b):
        if a[i][0] == b[j][0]:
            out.append((a[i][0], a[i][1] + b[j][1])); i += 1; j += 1
        elif a[i][0] < b[j][0]:
            i += 1
        else:
            j += 1
    return out

def search(index, n_docs, query, k):                 # returns [(doc, score)], best first
    lists = []
    for t in query.lower().split():
        post = index.get(t)
        if post is None:
            return []                                # AND: a missing term means no results
        idf = math.log(1 + n_docs / len(post))
        lists.append([(d, tf * idf) for d, tf in post])     # tf * idf
    lists.sort(key=len)                              # shortest list first
    cur = lists[0]
    for nxt in lists[1:]:
        cur = merge_and(cur, nxt)
    heap = []                                        # min-heap keeps the best k
    for d, s in cur:
        heapq.heappush(heap, (s, -d))
        if len(heap) > k:
            heapq.heappop(heap)
    return [(-nd, s) for s, nd in sorted(heap, reverse=True)]
--cpp
typedef map<string, vector<pair<int, int>>> Index;            // term -> list of {doc, tf}, sorted by doc

Index buildIndex(const vector<string>& docs) {
    Index index;
    for (size_t d = 0; d < docs.size(); d++) {
        map<string, int> tf; stringstream ss(docs[d]); string w;
        while (ss >> w) { transform(w.begin(), w.end(), w.begin(), ::tolower); tf[w]++; }
        for (auto& kv : tf) index[kv.first].push_back({(int)d, kv.second});
    }
    return index;
}

vector<pair<int, double>> mergeAnd(const vector<pair<int, double>>& a, const vector<pair<int, double>>& b) {   // two-pointer merge
    vector<pair<int, double>> out;
    size_t i = 0, j = 0;
    while (i < a.size() && j < b.size()) {
        if (a[i].first == b[j].first) { out.push_back({a[i].first, a[i].second + b[j].second}); i++; j++; }
        else if (a[i].first < b[j].first) i++;
        else j++;
    }
    return out;
}

vector<pair<int, double>> search(const Index& index, int nDocs, const string& query, int k) {   // {doc, score}, best first
    vector<vector<pair<int, double>>> lists;
    stringstream ss(query); string t;
    while (ss >> t) {
        transform(t.begin(), t.end(), t.begin(), ::tolower);
        auto it = index.find(t);
        if (it == index.end()) return {};                       // AND: a missing term means no results
        double idf = log(1.0 + (double)nDocs / it->second.size());
        vector<pair<int, double>> scored;
        for (auto& p : it->second) scored.push_back({p.first, p.second * idf});   // tf * idf
        lists.push_back(scored);
    }
    sort(lists.begin(), lists.end(), [](auto& x, auto& y) { return x.size() < y.size(); });   // shortest list first
    auto cur = lists[0];
    for (size_t x = 1; x < lists.size(); x++) cur = mergeAnd(cur, lists[x]);
    typedef pair<double, int> H;                                // {score, -doc}: the worst candidate on top
    priority_queue<H, vector<H>, greater<H>> heap;
    for (auto& r : cur) { heap.push({r.second, -r.first}); if ((int)heap.size() > k) heap.pop(); }   // keep the best k
    vector<pair<int, double>> out;
    while (!heap.empty()) { out.insert(out.begin(), {-heap.top().second, heap.top().first}); heap.pop(); }
    return out;
}
--go
import (
	"math"
	"sort"
	"strings"
)

type posting struct{ doc, tf int }
type hit struct {
	doc   int
	score float64
}

func buildIndex(docs []string) map[string][]posting { // term -> postings, sorted by doc
	index := map[string][]posting{}
	for d, text := range docs {
		tf := map[string]int{}
		for _, w := range strings.Fields(strings.ToLower(text)) {
			tf[w]++
		}
		for w, n := range tf {
			index[w] = append(index[w], posting{d, n})
		}
	}
	return index
}

func search(index map[string][]posting, nDocs int, query string, k int) []hit {
	var lists [][]hit
	for _, t := range strings.Fields(strings.ToLower(query)) {
		post, ok := index[t]
		if !ok { // AND: a missing term means no results
			return nil
		}
		idf := math.Log(1 + float64(nDocs)/float64(len(post)))
		var scored []hit
		for _, p := range post {
			scored = append(scored, hit{p.doc, float64(p.tf) * idf}) // tf * idf
		}
		lists = append(lists, scored)
	}
	if len(lists) == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	sort.Slice(lists, func(i, j int) bool { return len(lists[i]) < len(lists[j]) }) // shortest list first
	cur := lists[0]
	for _, l := range lists[1:] {
		cur = mergeAnd(cur, l)
	}
	sort.Slice(cur, func(i, j int) bool { // best score first, ties by doc id
		return cur[i].score > cur[j].score || cur[i].score == cur[j].score && cur[i].doc < cur[j].doc
	})
	return cur[:min(k, len(cur))] // a size-k heap would avoid sorting every match
}

func mergeAnd(a, b []hit) []hit { // two-pointer merge of lists sorted by doc
	var out []hit
	for i, j := 0, 0; i < len(a) && j < len(b); {
		switch {
		case a[i].doc == b[j].doc:
			out = append(out, hit{a[i].doc, a[i].score + b[j].score})
			i, j = i+1, j+1
		case a[i].doc < b[j].doc:
			i++
		default:
			j++
		}
	}
	return out
}

### crawler
--idea
A web crawler discovers pages by following links, the way you might explore a town by walking down every street you have not been on. Start with a few seed URLs, download each page, extract its links, and add the new ones to a to-do list. This is a graph search over the web, where pages are nodes and links are edges. Search engines use crawlers to collect the pages they index.
Three pieces make it work at scale. The to-do list (the frontier) is a priority queue, so the crawler can choose what to fetch next: shallow pages first (breadth-first search), important pages first, or stale pages that need refreshing. A "seen" set stops it from fetching the same URL twice, and with billions of URLs an exact set is too large, so a Bloom filter (a compact bit array that says "definitely new" or "probably seen") is used. And politeness rules limit how fast one site is fetched.
--build
1. Frontier: a min-heap of `(priority, order, url)`. With priority equal to the depth (number of links from a seed) the crawler does a BFS. The `order` counter breaks ties so entries pop in insertion order.
2. Seen filter: a Bloom filter with a bit array of `m` bits and `k` hash functions. `add(url)` sets `k` bits. `mightHave(url)` is true only if all `k` bits are set. It never forgets a URL that was added (no false negatives), and sometimes claims a new URL was seen (a false positive, which just skips one page).
3. Start: add every seed to the seen filter and push it with depth 0.
4. Loop: pop the best entry from the frontier, "fetch" the page (the model reads the link list from a dictionary) and record it in `fetched`. Stop when the frontier is empty or the page limit is reached.
5. For each link on the page, if the Bloom filter says it is new, add it to the filter and push it with `depth + 1`.
6. Real crawlers also respect `robots.txt`, keep a per-site queue with a delay between requests, normalise URLs (so `/a/` and `/a` are one page), detect near duplicates (see Near-duplicate detection), and re-crawl pages on a schedule based on how often they change.
--think
- Signal: exploring an unknown graph where the number of nodes is huge and you must not repeat work. The same pattern covers file system walks, dependency discovery, and web archiving.
- Heap versus plain queue: a queue gives BFS. A heap lets the priority be anything: link popularity, freshness, a score from a model, so the crawler spends its budget on the pages that matter. Depth-first would get lost in infinite generated pages ("crawler traps").
- Bloom filter trade-off: memory per URL is about 10 bits for a 1 percent false positive rate, versus tens of bytes for a stored string. The cost is that a small fraction of new pages are never fetched. Size the filter for the number of URLs you expect, because the false positive rate rises as it fills.
- At scale: partition URLs by host across machines (so politeness is local), and keep the frontier on disk with an in-memory front. Use the Consistent hashing idea so adding crawl machines does not reshuffle everything.
- Mistakes: marking a page seen only when fetched instead of when queued (the same URL is queued many times), ignoring politeness (the crawler gets blocked or harms the site), and trusting link text without normalising.
--cost
Each page costs O(links on it) heap and filter operations, O(log F) per heap operation for a frontier of F entries. The Bloom filter uses O(bits) memory, independent of URL length.
--java
static class Bloom {
    final boolean[] bits; final int k;
    Bloom(int m, int k) { bits = new boolean[m]; this.k = k; }
    private int spot(String s, int i) {                            // double hashing: h1 + i * h2
        long h = 0xcbf29ce484222325L;
        for (int c = 0; c < s.length(); c++) { h ^= s.charAt(c); h *= 0x100000001b3L; }
        h ^= h >>> 29; h *= 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9L; h ^= h >>> 32;
        long h1 = h & 0x7fffffffL, h2 = ((h >>> 32) & 0x7fffffffL) | 1;
        return (int) ((h1 + i * h2) % bits.length);
    }
    void add(String s) { for (int i = 0; i < k; i++) bits[spot(s, i)] = true; }
    boolean mightHave(String s) { for (int i = 0; i < k; i++) if (!bits[spot(s, i)]) return false; return true; }
}

static List<String> crawl(Map<String, List<String>> web, String seed, int limit) {
    Bloom seen = new Bloom(1 << 16, 3);
    PriorityQueue<Object[]> frontier = new PriorityQueue<>((a, b) -> {      // by depth, then insertion order
        int c = Integer.compare((int) a[0], (int) b[0]);
        return c != 0 ? c : Integer.compare((int) a[1], (int) b[1]);
    });
    int counter = 0;
    seen.add(seed); frontier.add(new Object[]{0, counter++, seed});
    List<String> fetched = new ArrayList<>();
    while (!frontier.isEmpty() && fetched.size() < limit) {
        Object[] e = frontier.poll();
        String url = (String) e[2];
        fetched.add(url);                                                   // "download" the page
        for (String link : web.getOrDefault(url, Collections.emptyList()))
            if (!seen.mightHave(link)) { seen.add(link); frontier.add(new Object[]{(int) e[0] + 1, counter++, link}); }
    }
    return fetched;
}
--py
import heapq

class Bloom:
    def __init__(self, m=1 << 16, k=3):
        self.m, self.k, self.bits = m, k, bytearray(m)

    def _spots(self, s):                             # double hashing: h1 + i * h2
        h = 0xcbf29ce484222325
        for ch in s:
            h = ((h ^ ord(ch)) * 0x100000001b3) & 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF
        h ^= h >> 29; h = (h * 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9) & 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF; h ^= h >> 32
        h1, h2 = h & 0x7fffffff, ((h >> 32) & 0x7fffffff) | 1
        return [(h1 + i * h2) % self.m for i in range(self.k)]

    def add(self, s):
        for p in self._spots(s): self.bits[p] = 1

    def might_have(self, s):
        return all(self.bits[p] for p in self._spots(s))

def crawl(web, seed, limit):                         # web: url -> list of links (stands in for downloading)
    seen, frontier, counter = Bloom(), [], 0
    seen.add(seed)
    heapq.heappush(frontier, (0, counter, seed))     # (depth, insertion order, url)
    fetched = []
    while frontier and len(fetched) < limit:
        depth, _, url = heapq.heappop(frontier)
        fetched.append(url)                          # "download" the page
        for link in web.get(url, []):
            if not seen.might_have(link):            # new, or a rare false positive that skips one page
                seen.add(link)
                counter += 1
                heapq.heappush(frontier, (depth + 1, counter, link))
    return fetched
--cpp
struct Bloom {
    vector<char> bits; int k;
    Bloom(size_t m = 1 << 16, int k = 3) : bits(m, 0), k(k) {}
    size_t spot(const string& s, int i) const {                   // double hashing: h1 + i * h2
        unsigned long long h = 0xcbf29ce484222325ULL;
        for (unsigned char c : s) { h ^= c; h *= 0x100000001b3ULL; }
        h ^= h >> 29; h *= 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9ULL; h ^= h >> 32;
        unsigned long long h1 = h & 0x7fffffff, h2 = ((h >> 32) & 0x7fffffff) | 1;
        return (h1 + i * h2) % bits.size();
    }
    void add(const string& s) { for (int i = 0; i < k; i++) bits[spot(s, i)] = 1; }
    bool mightHave(const string& s) const { for (int i = 0; i < k; i++) if (!bits[spot(s, i)]) return false; return true; }
};

vector<string> crawl(const map<string, vector<string>>& web, const string& seed, size_t limit) {
    Bloom seen;
    typedef tuple<int, int, string> E;                            // {depth, insertion order, url}
    priority_queue<E, vector<E>, greater<E>> frontier;
    int counter = 0;
    seen.add(seed); frontier.push({0, counter++, seed});
    vector<string> fetched;
    while (!frontier.empty() && fetched.size() < limit) {
        auto [depth, order, url] = frontier.top(); frontier.pop();
        fetched.push_back(url);                                   // "download" the page
        auto it = web.find(url);
        if (it == web.end()) continue;
        for (auto& link : it->second)
            if (!seen.mightHave(link)) { seen.add(link); frontier.push({depth + 1, counter++, link}); }
    }
    return fetched;
}
--go
import "container/heap"

type Bloom struct {
	bits []bool
	k    int
}

func newBloom(m, k int) *Bloom { return &Bloom{make([]bool, m), k} }

func (b *Bloom) spot(s string, i int) int { // double hashing: h1 + i * h2
	h := uint64(0xcbf29ce484222325)
	for c := 0; c < len(s); c++ {
		h ^= uint64(s[c])
		h *= 0x100000001b3
	}
	h ^= h >> 29
	h *= 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9
	h ^= h >> 32
	h1, h2 := h&0x7fffffff, (h>>32&0x7fffffff)|1
	return int((h1 + uint64(i)*h2) % uint64(len(b.bits)))
}
func (b *Bloom) add(s string) {
	for i := 0; i < b.k; i++ {
		b.bits[b.spot(s, i)] = true
	}
}
func (b *Bloom) mightHave(s string) bool {
	for i := 0; i < b.k; i++ {
		if !b.bits[b.spot(s, i)] {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

type page struct {
	depth, order int
	url          string
}
type frontier []page // a min-heap: by depth, then insertion order

func (f frontier) Len() int { return len(f) }
func (f frontier) Less(i, j int) bool {
	return f[i].depth < f[j].depth || f[i].depth == f[j].depth && f[i].order < f[j].order
}
func (f frontier) Swap(i, j int) { f[i], f[j] = f[j], f[i] }
func (f *frontier) Push(x any)   { *f = append(*f, x.(page)) }
func (f *frontier) Pop() any     { o := *f; x := o[len(o)-1]; *f = o[:len(o)-1]; return x }

func crawl(web map[string][]string, seed string, limit int) []string {
	seen := newBloom(1<<16, 3)
	counter := 1
	seen.add(seed)
	fr := &frontier{{0, 0, seed}}
	var fetched []string
	for fr.Len() > 0 && len(fetched) < limit {
		e := heap.Pop(fr).(page)
		fetched = append(fetched, e.url) // "download" the page
		for _, link := range web[e.url] {
			if !seen.mightHave(link) {
				seen.add(link)
				heap.Push(fr, page{e.depth + 1, counter, link})
				counter++
			}
		}
	}
	return fetched
}
### recsys
--idea
A recommendation system suggests items (films, products, songs) a person is likely to like, using what many people have done. There are two classic ways. Neighbour methods (collaborative filtering) find people whose ratings look like yours and suggest what they liked and you have not seen. Latent factor methods learn a short list of hidden numbers for every user and every item, so that the dot product of a user's numbers and an item's numbers predicts the rating.
The neighbour method is the k-nearest neighbours algorithm applied to rating vectors, with a similarity such as cosine (the angle between two vectors: 1 means the same direction, 0 means nothing in common). The latent factor method is trained by gradient descent: predict each known rating, measure the error, and nudge the numbers to shrink it. Either way, the final step is to rank the unseen items and keep the best few with a heap.
--build
1. Data: `ratings[user][item] = score`. Most users rate very few items, so store a dictionary per user (sparse), not a full table.
2. Similarity: `cosine(u, v) = sum over common items of u_i * v_i`, divided by the lengths of the two vectors. Users with no item in common have similarity 0.
3. Neighbours: compute the similarity of the target user to every other user and keep the `k` highest with a heap (see k-nearest neighbours and Top-k with a heap).
4. Predict a score for each item the user has not rated: the similarity-weighted average of the neighbours' ratings for it, `sum(sim * rating) / sum(|sim|)`, over the neighbours who rated it.
5. Recommend the `n` items with the highest predicted score, again with a size `n` heap.
6. Latent factors: give user `u` a vector `P[u]` and item `i` a vector `Q[i]`, each with `k` numbers. For every known rating `r`, compute `err = r - dot(P[u], Q[i])`, then move both vectors a small step to reduce the error: `P[u] += lr * (err * Q[i] - reg * P[u])` and the same for `Q[i]` (see Gradient descent). The `reg` term pulls numbers toward zero so the model does not memorise. Repeat for many epochs.
7. Predict any pair (even one never rated) as `dot(P[u], Q[i])`.
--think
- Signal: "people like you also liked", ranking items for a user, filling a sparse table of preferences. Start with neighbours for a baseline and explainability ("because you liked X"), move to latent factors for accuracy and scale.
- Cold start: a new user or item has no ratings. Fall back to popularity, content features, or asking the user for a few preferences.
- Implicit feedback (clicks, views, purchases) is more common than star ratings and is noisier. Treat it as confidence, not as a rating, and sample negatives.
- Scale: comparing a user with every other user is too slow, so real systems use approximate nearest neighbour search on the learned vectors (see Vector search and clustering) and rank in stages: fetch a few hundred candidates cheaply, then score them with a heavier model.
- Mistakes: measuring similarity on all items instead of common ones only, not centring ratings (a generous rater and a harsh rater look different even when they agree), recommending items already seen, and overfitting by training without regularisation or testing on held-out ratings.
--cost
Neighbour recommendation: O(users * items per user) to compare, plus the heap work. One factorisation epoch is O(ratings * k).
--java
static double cosine(Map<Integer, Double> a, Map<Integer, Double> b) {
    double dot = 0, na = 0, nb = 0;
    for (Map.Entry<Integer, Double> e : a.entrySet()) { na += e.getValue() * e.getValue(); Double r = b.get(e.getKey()); if (r != null) dot += e.getValue() * r; }
    for (double v : b.values()) nb += v * v;
    return na == 0 || nb == 0 ? 0 : dot / Math.sqrt(na * nb);
}

static List<Integer> recommend(Map<Integer, Map<Integer, Double>> ratings, int user, int kNeighbours, int n) {
    PriorityQueue<double[]> nb = new PriorityQueue<>((x, y) -> Double.compare(x[0], y[0]));    // keeps the k most similar users
    for (int o : ratings.keySet()) {
        if (o == user) continue;
        nb.add(new double[]{cosine(ratings.get(user), ratings.get(o)), o});
        if (nb.size() > kNeighbours) nb.poll();
    }
    Map<Integer, double[]> acc = new HashMap<>();                                              // item -> {sum sim*rating, sum |sim|}
    for (double[] s : nb) for (Map.Entry<Integer, Double> e : ratings.get((int) s[1]).entrySet()) {
        if (ratings.get(user).containsKey(e.getKey()) || s[0] <= 0) continue;                 // skip seen items and dissimilar users
        double[] a = acc.computeIfAbsent(e.getKey(), x -> new double[2]);
        a[0] += s[0] * e.getValue(); a[1] += Math.abs(s[0]);
    }
    PriorityQueue<double[]> best = new PriorityQueue<>((x, y) -> x[0] != y[0] ? Double.compare(x[0], y[0]) : Double.compare(y[1], x[1]));
    for (Map.Entry<Integer, double[]> e : acc.entrySet()) { best.add(new double[]{e.getValue()[0] / e.getValue()[1], e.getKey()}); if (best.size() > n) best.poll(); }
    LinkedList<Integer> out = new LinkedList<>();
    while (!best.isEmpty()) out.addFirst((int) best.poll()[1]);
    return out;
}

static double[][][] factorize(int[][] triples, double[] r, int nu, int ni, int k, double lr, double reg, int epochs, Random rnd) {
    double[][] P = new double[nu][k], Q = new double[ni][k];
    for (double[] row : P) for (int f = 0; f < k; f++) row[f] = 0.1 * rnd.nextDouble();
    for (double[] row : Q) for (int f = 0; f < k; f++) row[f] = 0.1 * rnd.nextDouble();
    for (int ep = 0; ep < epochs; ep++) for (int t = 0; t < triples.length; t++) {
        int u = triples[t][0], i = triples[t][1];
        double pred = 0;
        for (int f = 0; f < k; f++) pred += P[u][f] * Q[i][f];
        double err = r[t] - pred;
        for (int f = 0; f < k; f++) {                                       // gradient step on both vectors
            double pu = P[u][f], qi = Q[i][f];
            P[u][f] += lr * (err * qi - reg * pu);
            Q[i][f] += lr * (err * pu - reg * qi);
        }
    }
    return new double[][][]{P, Q};
}
--py
import heapq, math, random

def cosine(a, b):                                    # a, b: item -> rating
    dot = sum(r * b[i] for i, r in a.items() if i in b)
    na, nb = math.sqrt(sum(r * r for r in a.values())), math.sqrt(sum(r * r for r in b.values()))
    return dot / (na * nb) if na and nb else 0.0

def recommend(ratings, user, k_neighbours, n):       # ratings: user -> {item: rating}
    nbrs = heapq.nlargest(k_neighbours, ((cosine(ratings[user], ratings[o]), o) for o in ratings if o != user))
    acc = {}                                         # item -> [sum sim*rating, sum |sim|]
    for sim, o in nbrs:
        if sim <= 0:
            continue                                 # dissimilar users add nothing
        for item, r in ratings[o].items():
            if item not in ratings[user]:            # only unseen items
                a = acc.setdefault(item, [0.0, 0.0])
                a[0] += sim * r; a[1] += abs(sim)
    best = heapq.nlargest(n, ((s / w, -item) for item, (s, w) in acc.items()))
    return [-neg for _, neg in best]

def factorize(triples, nu, ni, k=2, lr=0.05, reg=0.01, epochs=500):   # triples: (user, item, rating)
    P = [[0.1 * random.random() for _ in range(k)] for _ in range(nu)]
    Q = [[0.1 * random.random() for _ in range(k)] for _ in range(ni)]
    for _ in range(epochs):
        for u, i, r in triples:
            err = r - sum(P[u][f] * Q[i][f] for f in range(k))
            for f in range(k):                       # gradient step on both vectors
                pu, qi = P[u][f], Q[i][f]
                P[u][f] += lr * (err * qi - reg * pu)
                Q[i][f] += lr * (err * pu - reg * qi)
    return P, Q
--cpp
double cosine(const map<int, double>& a, const map<int, double>& b) {
    double dot = 0, na = 0, nb = 0;
    for (auto& kv : a) { na += kv.second * kv.second; auto it = b.find(kv.first); if (it != b.end()) dot += kv.second * it->second; }
    for (auto& kv : b) nb += kv.second * kv.second;
    return na == 0 || nb == 0 ? 0 : dot / sqrt(na * nb);
}

vector<int> recommend(const map<int, map<int, double>>& ratings, int user, int kNeighbours, int n) {
    priority_queue<pair<double, int>, vector<pair<double, int>>, greater<pair<double, int>>> nb;   // keeps the k most similar users
    for (auto& kv : ratings) {
        if (kv.first == user) continue;
        nb.push({cosine(ratings.at(user), kv.second), kv.first});
        if ((int)nb.size() > kNeighbours) nb.pop();
    }
    map<int, pair<double, double>> acc;                       // item -> {sum sim*rating, sum |sim|}
    while (!nb.empty()) {
        auto [sim, o] = nb.top(); nb.pop();
        if (sim <= 0) continue;                               // dissimilar users add nothing
        for (auto& kv : ratings.at(o)) {
            if (ratings.at(user).count(kv.first)) continue;   // only unseen items
            acc[kv.first].first += sim * kv.second; acc[kv.first].second += fabs(sim);
        }
    }
    priority_queue<pair<double, int>, vector<pair<double, int>>, greater<pair<double, int>>> best;
    for (auto& kv : acc) { best.push({kv.second.first / kv.second.second, -kv.first}); if ((int)best.size() > n) best.pop(); }
    vector<int> out;
    while (!best.empty()) { out.insert(out.begin(), -best.top().second); best.pop(); }
    return out;
}

struct Rating { int u, i; double r; };
pair<vector<vector<double>>, vector<vector<double>>> factorize(const vector<Rating>& data, int nu, int ni, int k, double lr, double reg, int epochs, mt19937& rng) {
    uniform_real_distribution<double> unit(0.0, 0.1);
    vector<vector<double>> P(nu, vector<double>(k)), Q(ni, vector<double>(k));
    for (auto& row : P) for (double& x : row) x = unit(rng);
    for (auto& row : Q) for (double& x : row) x = unit(rng);
    for (int ep = 0; ep < epochs; ep++) for (auto& t : data) {
        double pred = 0;
        for (int f = 0; f < k; f++) pred += P[t.u][f] * Q[t.i][f];
        double err = t.r - pred;
        for (int f = 0; f < k; f++) {                         // gradient step on both vectors
            double pu = P[t.u][f], qi = Q[t.i][f];
            P[t.u][f] += lr * (err * qi - reg * pu);
            Q[t.i][f] += lr * (err * pu - reg * qi);
        }
    }
    return {P, Q};
}
--go
import (
	"math"
	"math/rand"
	"sort"
)

func cosine(a, b map[int]float64) float64 {
	dot, na, nb := 0.0, 0.0, 0.0
	for k, v := range a {
		na += v * v
		dot += v * b[k] // a missing rating is 0
	}
	for _, v := range b {
		nb += v * v
	}
	if na == 0 || nb == 0 {
		return 0
	}
	return dot / math.Sqrt(na*nb)
}

type scored struct {
	id    int
	score float64
}

func topN(xs []scored, n int) []scored { // best score first, ties by id
	sort.Slice(xs, func(i, j int) bool {
		return xs[i].score > xs[j].score || xs[i].score == xs[j].score && xs[i].id < xs[j].id
	})
	return xs[:min(n, len(xs))]
}

func recommend(ratings map[int]map[int]float64, user, kNeighbours, n int) []int {
	var sims []scored
	for o := range ratings {
		if o != user {
			sims = append(sims, scored{o, cosine(ratings[user], ratings[o])})
		}
	}
	acc := map[int][2]float64{}                 // item -> {sum sim*rating, sum |sim|}
	for _, s := range topN(sims, kNeighbours) { // the k most similar users
		for item, r := range ratings[s.id] {
			if _, seen := ratings[user][item]; seen || s.score <= 0 { // skip seen items and dissimilar users
				continue
			}
			a := acc[item]
			acc[item] = [2]float64{a[0] + s.score*r, a[1] + math.Abs(s.score)}
		}
	}
	var items []scored
	for item, a := range acc {
		items = append(items, scored{item, a[0] / a[1]})
	}
	var out []int
	for _, s := range topN(items, n) {
		out = append(out, s.id)
	}
	return out
}

// triples: {user, item} with rating r[t]. Returns the user factors P and item factors Q.
func factorize(triples [][2]int, r []float64, nu, ni, k int, lr, reg float64, epochs int, rnd *rand.Rand) (P, Q [][]float64) {
	init := func(rows int) [][]float64 {
		m := make([][]float64, rows)
		for i := range m {
			m[i] = make([]float64, k)
			for f := range m[i] {
				m[i][f] = 0.1 * rnd.Float64()
			}
		}
		return m
	}
	P, Q = init(nu), init(ni)
	for ep := 0; ep < epochs; ep++ {
		for t, tr := range triples {
			u, i := tr[0], tr[1]
			pred := 0.0
			for f := 0; f < k; f++ {
				pred += P[u][f] * Q[i][f]
			}
			err := r[t] - pred
			for f := 0; f < k; f++ { // gradient step on both vectors
				pu, qi := P[u][f], Q[i][f]
				P[u][f] += lr * (err*qi - reg*pu)
				Q[i][f] += lr * (err*pu - reg*qi)
			}
		}
	}
	return
}

### dedup
--idea
Near-duplicate detection finds documents that are almost the same: a web page copied with a different header, a re-posted article, a lightly edited file. Comparing every pair of documents word by word is far too slow when there are billions. The trick is to boil each document down to a tiny fingerprint, called a signature, such that two similar documents get similar signatures, and then compare only signatures.
First cut the document into overlapping chunks of `k` words, called shingles, so the document becomes a set. Two documents are similar when their sets overlap a lot, measured by the Jaccard similarity: the size of the overlap divided by the size of the union. MinHash estimates it cheaply. Hash every shingle with a random hash function and keep only the smallest hash value. The chance that two documents have the same smallest value is exactly their Jaccard similarity. Repeat with many hash functions, and the fraction of matching values is an estimate. To avoid comparing all pairs even of signatures, split each signature into bands and compare only documents that match in at least one whole band (locality sensitive hashing).
--build
1. Shingles: lowercase the text, split into words, and take every run of `k` consecutive words (here `k = 3`) as one shingle. The document is the set of its shingles.
2. Hash each shingle to a number (String hashing), reduced modulo a prime `P`.
3. Make a family of `n` hash functions `h_j(x) = (a_j * x + b_j) mod P` with fixed pseudo-random `a_j` and `b_j`. This is a cheap stand-in for `n` independent random permutations.
4. Signature: `sig[j] = min over shingles x of h_j(x)`. The signature has `n` numbers however long the document is.
5. Estimate similarity of two documents as the fraction of positions `j` where their signatures agree.
6. LSH banding: cut each signature into `bands` bands of `rows = n / bands` numbers. For each band, hash its numbers into a bucket (a Hash table keyed by the band index and its contents). Any two documents that land in the same bucket in some band are a candidate pair. Check candidates with the real signature or the full documents.
7. For exact copies, a plain hash of the whole text is enough, and a Bloom filter of seen hashes can pre-filter huge streams.
--think
- Signal: "find similar items in a huge collection" without comparing all pairs. Near-duplicate web pages, plagiarism, similar products, dedup of backups.
- The banding parameters tune a threshold. With `b` bands of `r` rows, two documents of similarity `s` become candidates with probability `1 - (1 - s^r)^b`, an S-shaped curve. More rows per band demand higher similarity and give fewer false candidates, more bands catch more true pairs.
- Alternatives: SimHash (a bit signature, compare by Hamming distance, used by search engines for web pages), and embeddings with vector search for meaning-based similarity (see Vector search and clustering).
- MinHash estimates have random error of about `1 / sqrt(n)` for `n` hash functions, so use 100 to 200 for a decent estimate.
- Mistakes: shingles of characters when words are meant (or the reverse), using a different hash family for different documents (signatures must be built with the same functions), and forgetting that very short documents have few shingles and noisy estimates.
--cost
Signature: O(shingles * n) per document. Candidate finding with banding: O(documents * bands) plus the cost of checking candidates.
--java
static final long P = 2147483647L;                                          // a prime; a*x stays below 2^62

static long shingleHash(String s) {
    long h = 0xcbf29ce484222325L;
    for (int i = 0; i < s.length(); i++) { h ^= s.charAt(i); h *= 0x100000001b3L; }
    return Long.remainderUnsigned(h ^ (h >>> 29), P);
}

static Set<Long> shingles(String text, int k) {                             // sets of hashed k-word shingles
    String[] w = text.toLowerCase().split("\\s+");
    Set<Long> out = new HashSet<>();
    for (int i = 0; i + k <= w.length; i++) out.add(shingleHash(String.join(" ", Arrays.copyOfRange(w, i, i + k))));
    if (out.isEmpty()) out.add(shingleHash(String.join(" ", w)));
    return out;
}

static long[] minhash(Set<Long> shs, int n) {                                // n hash functions h_j(x) = (a*x + b) mod P
    long[] sig = new long[n];
    for (int j = 0; j < n; j++) {
        long a = 1 + (j * 1103515245L + 12345L) % (P - 1), b = (j * 69069L + 1) % P, best = Long.MAX_VALUE;
        for (long x : shs) best = Math.min(best, (a * x + b) % P);
        sig[j] = best;
    }
    return sig;
}

static double similarity(long[] a, long[] b) {                               // fraction of equal positions
    int same = 0;
    for (int j = 0; j < a.length; j++) if (a[j] == b[j]) same++;
    return (double) same / a.length;
}

static Set<String> candidates(long[][] sigs, int bands) {                    // LSH: documents sharing any whole band
    int rows = sigs[0].length / bands;
    Set<String> pairs = new TreeSet<>();
    for (int b = 0; b < bands; b++) {
        Map<String, List<Integer>> buckets = new HashMap<>();
        for (int d = 0; d < sigs.length; d++)
            buckets.computeIfAbsent(Arrays.toString(Arrays.copyOfRange(sigs[d], b * rows, (b + 1) * rows)), x -> new ArrayList<>()).add(d);
        for (List<Integer> ds : buckets.values())
            for (int i = 0; i < ds.size(); i++) for (int j = i + 1; j < ds.size(); j++) pairs.add(ds.get(i) + "," + ds.get(j));
    }
    return pairs;
}
--py
P = 2147483647                                       # a prime; a*x stays below 2^62

def shingle_hash(s):
    h = 0xcbf29ce484222325
    for ch in s:
        h = ((h ^ ord(ch)) * 0x100000001b3) & 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF
